Orders paid on delivery carry the shop's fee for it ([ADR-076](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-076--a-shops-fee-for-cash-on-delivery-is-the-orders-own-amount-apart-from-delivery-in-its-total-and-the-cash-collected-said-beside-the-option-where-the-shopper-chooses)), and orders paid by transfer or online what it takes off for paying so, its own for each, after any code and to the rupee, each said beside its option ([ADR-077](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-077--something-off-for-paying-by-transfer-is-part-of-the-orders-discount-kept-apart-from-the-codes-off-the-items-after-any-code-to-the-rupee-said-where-the-shopper-chooses), [ADR-222](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-222--a-shop-may-take-something-off-orders-paid-online-as-it-may-off-those-paid-by-transfer-a-percentage-up-to-a-cap-or-an-amount-of-its-own-which-checkout-takes-off-the-items-after-any-code-and-the-order-keeps-apart)). Its rules may ask for an advance on cash on delivery, paid into its account: an amount, a share of the items or the delivery charge, on every order or above a total, said beside the option, the order waiting for it ([ADR-084](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-084--checkout-asks-for-the-advance-the-shops-rules-name-an-amount-a-share-of-the-items-or-the-delivery-charge-on-every-order-or-above-a-total-said-beside-cash-on-delivery)), or only to cities it names and of customers who refused parcels before, every city named and nobody looked up for the page ([ADR-089](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-089--a-shops-advance-may-be-asked-only-to-cities-it-names-and-of-customers-who-refused-parcels-before-checkout-names-every-city-and-says-of-whom-and-placing-applies-them-to-the-city-and-number-typed)); and of customers new to the shop, or of orders its risk rules score at a level it chooses or higher, which are asked it instead of waiting for review ([ADR-094](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-094--a-shops-advance-may-be-asked-only-of-customers-new-to-it-and-of-orders-its-risk-rules-score-high-such-an-order-is-asked-it-instead-of-waiting-for-review)). Orders its risk rules score at a limit of the shop's or above are not taken paid on delivery: placed, scored and undone, their page asks for a transfer instead, saying softly that the shop asks for the order to be paid in advance ([ADR-099](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-099--an-order-paid-on-delivery-that-the-shops-risk-rules-score-at-its-limit-or-above-is-not-taken-at-checkout-placed-scored-and-undone-its-page-asks-for-a-transfer-instead)). CHK-01's cash on delivery and bank transfer, PAY-02's bank transfer, its receipt and the shop's Raast ID, CHK-07, CHK-10's partial advance by the shop's rules, CHK-08's COD fee and prepaid incentive, CHK-19, CHK-02, its map pin where the customer's phone is at the address, added by the page's one script and kept near the city typed ([ADR-259](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-259--a-delivery-address-may-carry-a-pin-where-the-customers-phone-is-at-the-address-checkouts-and-customers-links-address-forms-add-it-by-the-pages-first-script-allowed-by-its-hash-it-is-kept-in-pakistan-and-near-the-city-typed-as-shopifys-latitude-and-longitude)), CH-07's checkout links, CHK-22, with how many working days delivery takes, everywhere and in each zone, which the cart and product pages say wherever it goes and checkout says for the city ([ADR-235](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-235--a-shop-may-say-how-many-working-days-delivery-takes-everywhere-and-in-each-of-its-delivery-zones-the-cart-and-product-pages-say-it-wherever-delivery-goes-from-the-fewest-days-anywhere-to-the-most-and-checkout-says-it-for-the-shoppers-city)), the MVP half of CHK-04, TAX-06's e-contract logs, CHK-18's limits on orders from one number or internet address, which checkout counts one at a time, saying why it takes no more ([ADR-087](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-087--checkout-takes-at-most-three-orders-a-day-from-one-mobile-number-and-twenty-an-hour-from-one-internet-address-counting-the-orders-it-placed-one-at-a-time)), and CHK-14's colours, logo and trust badges, which the shop picks from the platform's set and the page shows under its button where they hold ([ADR-086](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-086--a-shop-chooses-trust-badges-for-its-checkout-from-the-platforms-set-worded-in-english-and-urdu-and-shown-under-the-button-where-they-hold)). No order collects more than Rs 200,000 in cash at the door, the law's cap, whoever places it: the rest is paid in advance, or the order is refused. Where the shop has its account, checkout asks that advance itself, saying so wherever the order may pass the cap, and the cart says so too ([ADR-188](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-188--a-cart-past-the-laws-cap-on-cash-on-delivery-is-still-taken-on-delivery-where-the-shop-has-its-account-checkout-asks-in-advance-what-the-order-comes-to-past-rs-200000-or-the-shops-own-advance-where-that-is-more-says-so-wherever-the-order-may-pass-the-cap-and-the-cart-says-so-too)); otherwise it says so before the shopper types, offering bank transfer alone instead where the shop takes it ([ADR-058](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-058--no-order-collects-more-cash-on-delivery-than-the-law-allows-whoever-places-it-the-rest-is-paid-in-advance-or-the-order-is-not-placed)); TAX-07. From a risk score of the shop's, an order paid on delivery waits for a code sent to the number typed, on WhatsApp or by SMS, and keeps when its number was proved ([ADR-148](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-148--checkout-asks-a-shopper-paying-on-delivery-for-a-code-sent-to-the-number-they-typed-on-whatsapp-or-by-sms-where-the-shops-risk-rules-score-the-order-at-its-mark-a-digest-of-the-code-alone-is-kept-and-the-order-keeps-when-its-number-was-proved)), a browser that proved the number spared another for 30 days, but never to spend store credit ([ADR-199](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-199--a-browser-that-proved-a-number-with-a-code-at-a-shops-checkout-is-not-asked-for-another-for-it-there-for-30-days-where-the-shops-risk-rules-would-ask-it-keeps-a-random-token-in-a-cookie-for-checkouts-the-shop-a-digest-of-it-with-the-number-and-when-it-was-proved-spending-store-credit-still-asks-each-time)): CHK-09's rule-based half. Where the shop's payment gateway takes its currency, checkout offers paying online by card or wallet too, and in place of cash on delivery where that is refused: the order is placed to wait for its total, its thank-you page sends the shopper to the gateway, and the gateway sends them back to the checkout's address on the core, the page saying the payment is in ([ADR-152](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-152--checkout-offers-paying-online-where-the-shop-has-a-gateway-the-order-is-placed-to-wait-for-its-total-as-a-transfers-does-and-its-thank-you-page-sends-the-shopper-to-the-shops-gateway-which-sends-them-back-to-the-checkouts-address-on-the-core)): PAY-01 at checkout. The shopper may give an email too, kept with the order and the customer it makes, where the order's news goes as well ([ADR-181](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-181--a-shops-customers-hear-of-their-orders-by-email-too-where-they-gave-one-at-checkout-each-message-about-an-order-queues-a-copy-for-the-address-with-the-same-words-and-link-which-the-worker-sends-through-amazon-ses-from-hattis-address-under-the-shops-name-emails-cost-the-shop-nothing)). Once the shop has given store credit, the page offers paying with it: the shopper proves their number with a code, and the order is placed paid by as much as the number's credit covers, the cash at the door, the transfer or the payment online less by that much and its thank-you page saying so, or the page says the number has none ([ADR-186](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-186--a-shopper-pays-at-checkout-with-the-store-credit-their-number-has-once-they-prove-it-with-a-code-the-page-offers-it-once-the-shop-has-given-any-says-so-when-the-number-has-none-and-spends-what-the-credit-covers-of-the-order-as-it-is-placed-in-the-same-transaction)): ORD-09 at checkout. Under the email, a box for the shop's news and offers on each channel it chooses, WhatsApp until it does, never ticked beforehand: one ticked is the customer's consent as the order is placed, in its words ([ADR-187](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-187--a-shops-checkout-offers-a-box-for-its-news-and-offers-on-each-channel-it-chooses-whatsapp-until-it-does-unticked-until-the-shopper-ticks-it-a-box-ticked-records-the-customers-consent-as-the-order-is-placed-in-the-words-beside-it-where-the-number-or-email-typed-is-the-customers-own)): CUS-04 at checkout. Discount codes are in their own row. Not yet: abandoned checkouts. Payment links many customers open ([ADR-248](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-248--a-payment-link-the-shop-shares-once-opens-a-checkout-of-each-customers-own-with-its-items-and-discount-code-until-staff-close-it-its-time-passes-or-its-orders-run-out-one-taken-prepaid-alone-offers-no-cash-on-delivery)): PAY-04 |
| Discounts (ahead of the MVP) | ✅ Codes, at checkout and in the cart | Discount codes through the Admin API, as Shopify's basic and free-shipping codes are: a percentage or an amount off an order's items, or free delivery, with a minimum, dates, a limit on uses and one use a customer, matched in any letter case, in a pricing module of their own ([ADR-062](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-062--discount-codes-are-the-pricing-modules-a-percentage-or-an-amount-off-an-orders-items-or-free-delivery-matched-in-any-letter-case)). Checkout's page takes a code in a form of its own and the cart keeps it; the page shows what it takes off, and the order is placed with it, its use counted in the order's transaction, never past its limit or twice by a customer meant to use it once, and kept with the order ([ADR-063](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-063--a-shoppers-discount-code-is-kept-with-their-cart-and-counted-with-the-order-placed-with-it-in-the-orders-transaction)). Shopify's `/discount/` links keep a code with the shopper's cart, one begun for it if need be, and send them on; the cart takes Shopify's `discount` from scripts, and its JSON and Liquid's `cart` say what the code takes off, which Hatti Base's cart page and drawer show, in English and Urdu ([ADR-064](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-064--discount-links-keep-their-code-with-the-shoppers-cart-one-begun-for-it-if-need-be-and-a-cart-says-of-a-code-only-whether-it-applies)). CHK-06's MVP half. Not yet: codes for some products or collections, automatic discounts, combining codes, codes on staff's orders and drafts, and the admin's screens |
| Ads (ahead of V1) | ✅ Catalog feeds, Meta's conversions API and Meta's pixel | Each shop's catalog feed, which Google Merchant Center and Meta's catalogs fetch for free listings, Shopping and catalog ads, at its storefront's own address ([ADR-142](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-142--a-shops-catalog-feed-is-its-storefronts-at-its-own-address-an-item-for-each-variant-of-its-products-with-an-image-in-googles-rss-which-metas-catalogs-read-too-made-from-its-documents-a-chunk-at-a-time); see Storefront documents). Orders placed through checkout go to Meta's conversions API from the worker as they are placed, confirmed and delivered, the shop choosing which is Meta's Purchase, so that Meta learns from the orders that turned out rather than those refused at the door: each with the order's total and its items by variant, as the feed names them, its customer's details hashed as Meta asks, where they placed it from and the ad's click that brought them ([ADR-139](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-139--a-shoppers-browser-keeps-the-visits-that-brought-them-the-first-and-the-last-from-elsewhere-checkout-passes-them-on-and-the-order-keeps-them-as-shopifys-customer-journey)); each moment waits in Postgres until Meta takes it, is tried again while Meta cannot, fails with what Meta said when it refuses it, and expires unsent after Meta's seven days ([ADR-143](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-143--orders-placed-through-checkout-go-to-metas-conversions-api-from-the-worker-as-they-are-placed-confirmed-and-delivered-the-shop-choosing-which-is-purchase-each-moment-waits-in-postgres-until-meta-takes-it-or-its-seven-days-are-up)). The Admin API connects the shop's dataset, its token sealed, never shown again and its changes audited, and lists every moment with how sending it went. While it is connected, shoppers' pages load Meta's pixel: `PageView`, `ViewContent` for a product's page by the feed's IDs, and `AddToCart` and `InitiateCheckout` from the forms shoppers send; orders' events go from the server alone, checkout's page running no scripts, and the order keeps the pixel's browser and click IDs, which its events send ([ADR-144](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-144--a-shops-storefront-loads-its-meta-pixel-while-meta-is-connected-for-the-steps-shoppers-take-before-checkout-orders-go-from-the-server-alone-each-keeping-the-pixels-browser-and-click-ids-for-them)). MKT-11 for Meta and Google; MKT-10 for Meta's conversions API and pixel. Not yet: TikTok's and Google's conversions, a consent banner, ad spend |
| Couriers' cash (ahead of the MVP) | ✅ Statements and what is owed | What couriers owe the shop for cash on delivery: on delivered orders not yet paid, by courier and by days since delivery, and what is still on its way ([ADR-066](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-066--what-couriers-owe-is-worked-out-from-the-orders-when-asked-delivered-cash-on-delivery-orders-not-yet-paid-by-courier-and-by-days-since-delivery)). Couriers' remittance statements imported as the CSV they send, their columns found by the names couriers use: each line matched to a parcel by its tracking number and its cash received on the parcel's order, at most what it owes, in full or in part, in one transaction; lines that match no parcel, a parcel paid for before, or an order that owes nothing, kept to look into, with the courier's charges and the tax withheld; a dry run first, and a statement's reference taken once ([ADR-067](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-067--couriers-remittance-statements-are-imported-whole-into-a-logistics-module-each-lines-cash-received-on-its-parcels-order-at-most-what-the-order-owes-and-a-parcels-cash-once)). Each line's charges kept on its parcel, out and back, and a statement taken once by its lines too, however it was saved, one at a time ([ADR-088](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-088--a-parcel-keeps-what-couriers-statements-charged-for-it-which-cod-health-adds-up-for-those-that-came-back-a-statement-with-the-lines-of-one-imported-before-is-refused)). Cash for a parcel the courier lost pays the parcel's claim, filed or not, and each statement keeps what of its cash did so ([ADR-093](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-093--a-claim-on-the-courier-that-lost-a-parcel-is-the-parcels-followed-until-the-courier-pays-it-or-refuses-it-a-statements-cash-for-a-lost-parcel-pays-its-claim-filed-or-not)). The MVP half of COD-10. Not yet: couriers' APIs, the ledger of fees and tax credits, dispute sheets, and the admin's screens |
| Courier bookings (MVP) | ✅ PostEx and Leopards, first slice | The shop's own accounts with couriers, their credentials sealed for each account and never shown again, one the default; orders booked with one, up to 250 at a time, each on its own: refused with why when it could not ship now, has part shipped or is being booked already, else waiting until the worker books it with the courier, keeps the courier's tracking number and ships the order as a parcel with it, the cash it owes collected at the door; a courier that cannot take it asked again for a day, and one that refuses it saying why; then each parcel asked about as often as where it is calls for, the courier's words read through mappings kept as data, delivered and coming back marked on the parcel, which tells the customer ([ADR-149](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-149--shops-book-orders-with-their-own-courier-accounts-their-credentials-sealed-for-each-account-each-booking-waits-in-postgres-until-the-worker-books-it-through-the-couriers-adapter-keeps-the-couriers-number-before-shipping-the-order-with-it-and-follows-the-parcel-by-asking-the-couriers-words-read-through-mappings-kept-as-data)). PostEx's API, and Leopards', its parcels' cities by its own IDs and its parcels asked about fifty at a time ([ADR-162](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-162--leopards-is-the-second-courier-shops-book-with-through-the-same-adapter-the-accounts-key-and-password-in-each-requests-body-a-parcels-city-by-leopards-own-id-from-its-list-of-cities-kept-a-day-the-accounts-own-shipper-unless-a-shipper-id-is-given-its-parcels-asked-about-fifty-at-a-time-and-its-words-read-through-rows-of-data)), and a test courier outside production. Labels for booked parcels, the courier's tracking number as a Code 128 barcode with the cash it collects, one to a 4×6 inch label or four to a sheet of A4, and each account's load sheet of the parcels waiting for pickup, for the rider to sign ([ADR-150](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-150--couriers-labels-and-load-sheets-are-hattis-own-printed-pages-a-booked-parcels-label-carries-the-couriers-tracking-number-as-a-code-128-barcode-and-the-cash-the-courier-was-asked-to-collect-one-to-a-46-inch-label-or-four-to-a-sheet-of-a4-and-an-accounts-load-sheet-lists-its-parcels-waiting-to-be-picked-up-for-the-shop-and-the-rider-to-sign)). Each change a courier says is a step of the parcel's way, recorded once, which its customer's page shows, the latest first, with the courier's words; staff record the steps of couriers Hatti does not follow ([ADR-160](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-160--each-parcels-way-is-kept-step-by-step-as-shopifys-fulfillmentevent-its-couriers-changes-recorded-once-from-the-workers-tracking-and-staffs-for-couriers-hatti-does-not-follow-the-orders-page-shows-them-the-latest-first-in-english-and-urdu-the-shipped-message-links-that-page-and-a-parcel-out-for-delivery-with-cash-to-collect-tells-its-customer-what-to-keep-ready)). A parcel's city is booked as its courier names it: the shop's own name for it, else Hatti's, else the courier's list's, matched through Pakistan's names for the city and their aliases; a city the list doesn't name fails its booking with the courier's nearest names, and the name staff choose is kept for the shop's next parcel ([ADR-233](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-233--a-parcels-city-is-booked-as-its-courier-names-it-the-shops-own-name-for-it-else-hattis-else-the-couriers-lists-matched-through-pakistans-names-for-the-city-and-their-aliases-a-city-the-list-names-none-of-fails-its-booking-with-the-couriers-nearest-names-and-the-name-staff-give-is-kept-for-the-shops-next-parcel)). SHP-01's first two couriers, SHP-02's booking, labels and load sheets, SHP-03's city names, a name three shops gave a city alike, none giving another, being every shop's ([ADR-260](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-260--a-name-for-a-city-with-a-courier-that-three-shops-gave-alike-which-no-shop-gave-otherwise-is-every-shops-after-the-shops-own-and-hattis-hattis-people-keep-hattis-names-with-a-command-which-settles-a-city-shops-named-wrong)), SHP-04's tracking and SHP-05's page. Pickups through PostEx's and Leopards' APIs, each keeping its parcels and the courier's sheet ([ADR-253](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-253--a-courier-accounts-parcels-waiting-to-be-picked-up-are-handed-to-its-courier-through-its-api-postexs-load-sheet-for-its-pickup-address-and-leopards-naming-the-rider-who-takes-them-each-pickup-keeps-its-parcels-and-the-couriers-sheet-and-a-parcel-its-rider-missed-goes-in-the-next-a-day-later)). Not yet: TCS and Trax, couriers' own airway bills, and couriers' own histories with their times |
| Payments online (MVP) | ✅ Safepay, JazzCash, Easypaisa, PayFast, Bank Alfalah's, HBL's and Baadmay's buy now, pay later, at checkout, on orders' pages, and Safepay's and JazzCash's refunds | The shop's own accounts with payment gateways, Safepay first, their credentials sealed for each account and never shown again, in the gateway's sandbox or its real environment, connected and changed by owners and managers who proved who they are lately ([ADR-151](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-151--shops-take-payments-online-through-their-own-gateway-accounts-safepay-first-their-credentials-sealed-for-each-account-an-order-waiting-for-its-money-offers-to-take-it-on-its-page-a-session-is-recorded-before-the-customer-leaves-for-the-gateway-and-the-gateways-signed-return-or-webhook-whichever-comes-first-records-it-paid-once-and-pays-what-the-order-owes-of-it-a-sandboxs-payments-pay-nothing)). An order waiting for its money, a transfer's total or a cash-on-delivery order's advance, offers to take it online on its page: a session recorded before the customer leaves for the gateway's checkout, then the gateway's signed return or webhook, whichever comes first, records it paid once and pays what the order owes of it, anything beyond on its timeline to give back; a sandbox's payments pay nothing. `paymentSessions` lists an order's. Checkout offers paying online beside cash on delivery and transfer: the order placed waits for its total, `ONLINE` in the Admin API, and its thank-you page sends the shopper to the gateway, which sends them back to the checkout's address on the core ([ADR-152](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-152--checkout-offers-paying-online-where-the-shop-has-a-gateway-the-order-is-placed-to-wait-for-its-total-as-a-transfers-does-and-its-thank-you-page-sends-the-shopper-to-the-shops-gateway-which-sends-them-back-to-the-checkouts-address-on-the-core)). Refunds by `ONLINE` give what was paid online back through the gateway that took it, as far as its adapter can, Safepay a payment whole: recorded before the gateway is asked and written on the order once it says it is sent, a refusal said, and one without an answer holding its amount until staff settle it from the gateway's dashboard ([ADR-153](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-153--money-paid-online-goes-back-through-the-gateway-that-took-it-as-far-as-its-adapter-can-give-it-back-safepay-a-payment-whole-each-refund-is-recorded-before-the-gateway-is-asked-and-written-on-its-order-once-the-gateway-says-it-is-sent-a-refusal-is-said-and-a-refund-without-an-answer-holds-its-amount-until-staff-settle-it-from-the-gateways-dashboard)): PAY-06. JazzCash's hosted checkout too, its wallets, cards and vouchers: the customer's browser posts a form signed with the account's integrity salt to JazzCash's page, from a page with a button, as the pages run no scripts, and JazzCash posts the outcome back signed the same way; the form is never kept ([ADR-163](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-163--jazzcash-is-the-second-gateway-shops-take-payments-through-by-its-hosted-checkout-the-customers-browser-posts-a-form-signed-with-the-accounts-integrity-salt-to-jazzcashs-page-from-a-page-of-hattis-with-a-button-as-these-pages-run-no-scripts-and-jazzcash-posts-the-outcome-back-signed-the-same-way-the-form-is-never-kept-and-nothing-is-given-back-through-its-api)). A payment whose customer never came back is asked after through JazzCash's status inquiry or Safepay's reporter, hourly for two days, and recorded paid once the gateway vouches for it ([ADR-208](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-208--a-payment-started-online-whose-customer-never-came-back-is-asked-after-the-worker-asks-the-gateways-status-inquiry-jazzcashs-first-from-a-quarter-of-an-hour-after-it-began-at-most-once-an-hour-for-two-days-and-records-one-the-gateway-vouches-for-paid-through-the-inquiry)). Safepay's too ([ADR-210](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-210--safepays-trackers-are-asked-after-as-jazzcashs-payments-are-through-its-reporter-with-the-accounts-secret-key-its-answer-which-safepay-does-not-sign-is-believed-as-it-comes-from-safepays-own-api-and-only-naming-the-accounts-api-key-and-the-tracker-asked-about)). Easypaisa's hosted checkout too: a form encrypted with the store's hash key, then the token it sends the customer back with posted on to its next page, and its return, which it does not sign, believed only once its inquiry, asked at once, says the payment is made ([ADR-214](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-214--easypaisa-is-the-third-gateway-shops-take-payments-through-by-its-hosted-checkout-the-customers-browser-posts-a-form-encrypted-with-the-stores-hash-key-to-its-page-and-the-token-it-comes-back-with-to-its-next-and-its-return-which-it-does-not-sign-is-believed-only-once-its-inquiry-asked-at-once-with-the-accounts-api-credentials-says-the-payment-is-made)). A shop's live accounts are offered together, the customer paying through the one they choose ([ADR-219](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-219--customers-choose-among-the-shops-gateways-each-live-account-that-takes-the-orders-currency-is-offered-on-its-page-and-checkouts-thank-you-page-in-the-order-the-shop-added-them-and-the-payment-starts-through-the-one-chosen)), in the order the shop puts them ([ADR-221](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-221--a-shop-puts-its-gateways-in-the-order-its-customers-are-offered-them-the-admin-api-takes-all-its-live-accounts-at-once-those-connected-before-keep-the-order-they-were-connected-in-and-one-connected-later-goes-last)). Checkout takes the shop's own discount off orders paid online, a percentage up to a cap or an amount, as off those paid by transfer ([ADR-222](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-222--a-shop-may-take-something-off-orders-paid-online-as-it-may-off-those-paid-by-transfer-a-percentage-up-to-a-cap-or-an-amount-of-its-own-which-checkout-takes-off-the-items-after-any-code-and-the-order-keeps-apart)). Baadmay's buy now, pay later too: the order, its items and its customer in its page's address, and its return, which it does not sign, believed only once its order status, asked at once, names the order and the amount paid ([ADR-226](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-226--baadmays-buy-now-pay-later-is-a-gateway-shops-take-payments-through-the-order-its-items-and-its-customer-go-to-its-page-in-the-address-and-its-return-which-it-does-not-sign-is-believed-only-once-its-order-status-asked-at-once-names-the-order-and-the-amount-paid)). PayFast's hosted checkout too: a token asked for the basket with the secured key, then a form with it posted to its page, and its return and its word at the webhook, in the address too, believed by their validation hash ([ADR-227](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-227--payfast-is-a-gateway-shops-take-payments-through-an-access-token-asked-for-the-basket-and-its-amount-with-the-secured-key-then-a-form-with-the-token-posted-to-its-page-its-return-and-its-word-at-the-webhook-which-may-come-in-the-address-believed-by-their-validation-hash)). Bank Alfalah's too: a handshake hashed with the account's two keys, then a form with its token posted to its page, and its return, which it does not sign, believed only once its order status, asked at once, says the payment is made ([ADR-228](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-228--bank-alfalahs-payment-gateway-is-one-shops-take-payments-through-a-handshake-whose-request-is-hashed-with-the-accounts-two-keys-then-a-form-with-its-token-hashed-the-same-way-posted-to-its-page-its-return-which-it-does-not-sign-believed-only-once-its-order-status-asked-at-once-says-the-payment-is-made)). HBL's too: a session asked for with the order under a key of the request's own, which HBL's public key wraps with the password, and its return, encrypted to the shop's own public key, believed once the shop's private key opens it to a reference of Hatti's ([ADR-229](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-229--hbls-payment-gateway-is-one-shops-take-payments-through-a-session-asked-for-with-the-order-encrypted-under-a-key-of-the-requests-own-which-hbls-public-key-wraps-with-the-password-its-return-encrypted-to-the-shops-own-public-key-believed-once-the-shops-private-key-opens-it-to-a-reference-of-hattis)). JazzCash gives back what a card or its wallet paid, by how the payment says it was made, a wallet's with the MPIN the shop gives ([ADR-255](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-255--what-a-card-or-a-jazzcash-wallet-paid-goes-back-through-jazzcashs-refunds-a-wallets-with-the-mpin-the-shop-gives-a-payment-keeps-how-its-customer-paid-as-its-gateway-said-and-a-voucher-paid-at-a-shop-goes-back-another-way-claimed-whole-until-tried-against-its-sandbox)). Not yet: other gateways' refunds, paying before the order is placed, hearing of refunds from webhooks, and an inquiry of the gateway or reconciliation against its settlements |
| Billing (MVP) | ✅ Plans and message credits, first slice | What shops pay Hatti: Free, or Starter, Growth or Pro in rupees by the month or the year, a year at ten months' price, a shop without a subscription on Free. The owner chooses, having signed in lately: a bigger plan is invoiced now, less what is left of the period it cuts short, and begins once paid; a smaller one, or Free, when the period ends. Invoices, numbered across Hatti, are paid through Hatti's own Safepay account, recorded before the owner leaves for it and paid once by its signed return or webhook; the worker invoices each period a week ahead, and a week unpaid puts the shop on Free ([ADR-154](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-154--shops-pay-hatti-for-a-plan-in-rupees-by-the-month-or-the-year-through-hattis-own-payment-gateway-account-a-bigger-plan-begins-once-its-invoice-is-paid-less-what-is-left-of-the-period-it-cuts-short-a-smaller-one-when-the-period-ends-each-period-is-invoiced-a-week-ahead-and-a-week-unpaid-puts-the-shop-on-free-other-modules-ask-each-plans-limits-through-a-port)). Staff and locations are held to the plan's limits through a port the billing module provides. Credit for the shop's messages is bought the same way, with an invoice of its own, and each message is paid from it as it goes, at what it costs Hatti and Hatti's fee, in a ledger kept beside the balance ([ADR-155](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-155--a-shops-messages-are-paid-from-credit-in-rupees-it-buys-from-hatti-with-an-invoice-of-its-own-each-is-charged-as-it-is-sent-at-what-it-costs-hatti-and-hattis-fee-in-a-ledger-kept-beside-the-balance-a-message-the-credit-cannot-pay-for-waits-and-a-code-is-not-sent-and-what-whatsapp-could-not-deliver-is-given-back)). The owner hears at Hatti's cost, on WhatsApp at the shop's alerts number and by email at the address their account proved, in their own language, of each renewal's invoice, of a plan ended unpaid, and of credit fallen below Rs 100 ([ADR-195](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-195--a-shops-owner-hears-of-its-bills-with-hatti-by-email-too-at-the-address-their-account-proved-and-in-their-own-language-from-hattis-own-address-the-worker-finds-them-through-identitys-functions-for-the-shop-alone-and-queues-each-email-with-the-shops-messages-at-hattis-cost-with-an-alerts-number-or-without), [ADR-169](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-169--hatti-tells-a-shop-on-whatsapp-at-the-number-it-gives-for-hattis-alerts-when-its-plans-next-period-is-invoiced-when-its-plan-ends-unpaid-and-when-its-message-credit-falls-below-rs-100-each-once-queued-with-its-messages-from-billings-events-at-hattis-cost-whatever-its-credit-and-never-turned-off)). Invoices are paid by transfer or Raast into Hatti's own account too: the owner gives the transfer's reference, and Hatti's people confirm it, which pays the invoice, or refuse it, the owner told either way ([ADR-254](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-254--a-shop-pays-hattis-invoice-by-transfer-or-raast-into-hattis-own-bank-account-its-owner-giving-the-transfers-reference-hattis-people-confirm-it-once-they-find-it-which-pays-the-invoice-as-a-gateways-payment-does-with-what-its-other-payments-brought-or-refuse-it-saying-why-and-the-owner-hears-either-way)). BIL-01 but auto-debit and Raast's request-to-pay; BIL-03's message credits. Not yet: sales tax on Hatti's invoices (BIL-02), auto-debit, Raast's request-to-pay, add-ons and AI credits, owners reminded once a period ends unpaid (BIL-04), other features gated by plan, and Free's order limit |
| Files (ahead of the MVP) | ✅ Uploads through the Admin API | Files a shop uploads, kept in object storage under its own prefix: R2 through the S3 API, signed with Signature Version 4, and in development a directory the API serves at `/storage`. Shopify's `stagedUploadsCreate` signs where each upload goes, for its exact size and type; the client sends the bytes straight there; `fileCreate` makes a file of it once it is in and its first bytes are its type's, or removes it; `files` and `fileDelete` under Shopify's file scopes. Nothing is public: a file is shown through a URL signed for an hour ([ADR-079](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-079--files-are-kept-in-object-storage-under-each-shops-prefix-uploaded-straight-there-through-urls-the-admin-api-signs-and-shown-only-through-short-lived-signed-urls-a-directory-stands-in-for-r2-in-development)). Customers' receipts for transfers come through their orders' pages into the same storage, the orders module's ([ADR-080](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-080--a-customer-sends-the-receipt-of-their-transfer-through-their-orders-page-in-a-form-the-core-reads-and-keeps-in-storage-by-order-the-shop-sees-it-with-the-order)). The shop's logo is one of its files, chosen as its brand's, which the checkout's page shows ([ADR-081](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-081--a-shops-logo-is-one-of-its-files-chosen-as-its-brands-the-checkouts-page-shows-it-in-place-of-the-shops-name-through-a-url-signed-for-an-hour-that-the-pages-policy-allows-alone)). Its square logo is another, which its link page shows at its top, both served by the API at addresses of their own ([ADR-205](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-205--a-shops-brand-has-shopifys-square-logo-beside-its-logo-one-of-its-files-served-by-the-api-at-an-address-of-its-own-the-shops-document-names-where-each-logo-is-served-each-address-naming-its-file-and-the-link-page-shows-the-square-logo-else-the-logo-at-its-top)). Products' images come from the shop's uploads too, checked and kept by the worker, and served at the sizes browsers ask for ([ADR-158](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-158--hatti-keeps-products-images-itself-the-worker-reads-each-from-the-shops-upload-or-fetches-it-from-its-url-never-reaching-a-private-network-checks-it-and-keeps-a-clean-copy-without-its-metadata-at-most-4096-pixels-a-side-the-api-serves-it-at-nine-widths-in-avif-webp-or-its-own-format-each-made-the-first-time-it-is-asked-for-and-kept-and-an-image-goes-from-storage-and-the-edge-with-its-media)). Not yet: files' own images resized |
| Setup checklist (ahead of the MVP) | ✅ Done, first slice | What a new shop has left to set up before it sells, worked out when asked from what each module keeps, in one transaction ([ADR-095](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-095--the-setup-checklist-is-worked-out-when-asked-from-what-each-module-keeps-in-one-transaction-a-step-is-done-while-what-it-asks-for-holds)): products on sale, delivery charges, a courier account, a gateway's account taking real money or a bank account for transfers and advances ([ADR-232](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-232--the-setup-checklist-asks-for-a-courier-account-the-test-couriers-aside-and-counts-a-payment-gateways-account-in-its-production-as-a-way-to-be-paid-ahead-as-it-counts-a-bank-account)), the four policies shoppers look for, a logo, a WhatsApp number, and the store open; each step done while it holds, with how far along those of many things are. ONB-02 but sharing the store, which waits for the admin app |
| Sales tax (MVP) | ✅ Done | The shop's sales tax, kept by a tax module of its own: one rate, or none, included in its prices, as Pakistan's consumer laws ask prices to be shown, and in its delivery charges and fee for paying on delivery if it says so ([ADR-096](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-096--sales-tax-is-included-in-prices-at-a-rate-the-tax-module-keeps-each-order-keeps-the-tax-in-it-as-it-was-placed-line-by-line-and-in-its-delivery)). Variants are taxed unless the shop says otherwise, Shopify's `taxable`, which the product import reads. Each order keeps the tax in it as it was placed: each line's, after its share of the discount, rounded line by line, and its charges'. Checkout's page says what of its total is tax, worked out as placing will; the placed page, invoices and customers' order pages give a line per rate under the total, in English and Urdu; the API gives Shopify's `taxesIncluded`, `totalTax` and `taxLines`, and the export the order's taxes and each line's. Categories: rates of their own for products whose variants' Shopify tax code names them, every other taxed variant at the shop's rate, a line a rate on every page ([ADR-097](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-097--tax-categories-are-the-shops-codes-with-rates-of-their-own-which-variants-name-by-shopifys-tax-code-every-other-variant-it-taxes-is-at-the-shops-rate)). Refunds keep their share of their order's tax, all of it once the whole order is refunded, orders what they keep after them, and the sales report the tax its sales include ([ADR-105](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-105--a-refund-keeps-its-share-of-its-orders-sales-tax-the-orders-tax-in-all-it-has-refunded-less-what-the-refunds-before-it-gave-back-the-sales-report-adds-up-the-tax-its-sales-include)), apart from its other amounts, which leave it out as Shopify's do ([ADR-117](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-117--the-sales-report-leaves-out-the-sales-tax-its-amounts-include-as-shopifys-does-worked-out-from-the-tax-each-order-keeps-the-tax-said-apart-and-added-back-in-total-sales)). Drafts say the tax their prices include before they are placed, as the order they become will keep it, in the API and on their links' pages, and once placed their order's ([ADR-106](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-106--a-draft-says-the-sales-tax-its-prices-include-an-open-ones-at-the-shops-rates-now-as-placing-it-would-work-it-out-a-completed-ones-as-its-order-keeps-it)). The shop's NTN and sales tax registration number, kept with its tax settings as FBR writes them, which its invoices name: with the second, they are sales tax invoices, which say their value without the tax too ([ADR-190](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-190--a-shops-ntn-and-sales-tax-registration-number-are-kept-with-its-tax-settings-as-fbr-writes-them-and-its-invoices-name-them-with-a-sales-tax-registration-number-they-are-sales-tax-invoices-which-say-their-value-without-the-tax-too)). TAX-01, CHK-17, and TAX-02's numbers. Not yet: the rest of the merchant's tax profile (TAX-02), a series of invoices of their own (TAX-05) and FBR's digital invoicing (TAX-04) |
| Messaging (MVP) | ✅ Order updates, first slice | What a shop's customers are told about their orders: placed, each parcel shipped with its tracking number and delivered, and cancelled, on WhatsApp from Hatti's shared number or by SMS, in English or Urdu, as the shop chooses ([ADR-146](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-146--a-shops-customers-hear-of-their-orders-from-hattis-shared-whatsapp-number-or-by-sms-where-the-shop-saves-or-whatsapp-cannot-deliver-each-message-waits-in-postgres-queued-once-from-the-orders-events-until-the-worker-sends-it-and-whatsapps-webhook-follows-it-and-hears-customers-ask-to-stop)). Each message is queued once from the order's events and sent by the worker, tried again while its channel cannot take it, by SMS when WhatsApp cannot deliver it or has not within 15 minutes, followed to delivery through WhatsApp's signed webhook, and never sent to a customer who said "STOP" or "band karo" to the shop; the Admin API lists them with how sending went. A cash-on-delivery order waiting for its customer asks them to confirm it, with Confirm, Cancel and Change address buttons and its link, by SMS with the link when WhatsApp cannot deliver it; their answer, heard through the webhook, confirms or cancels it in the worker as their link would ([ADR-147](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-147--a-cash-on-delivery-order-waiting-for-its-customer-asks-them-on-whatsapp-to-confirm-it-with-confirm-cancel-and-change-address-buttons-and-its-link-their-answer-comes-through-the-webhook-as-an-event-and-the-worker-confirms-or-cancels-the-order-as-their-link-would)). One who has not answered three hours on is asked once more, in the shop's calling hours, with the same buttons and link ([ADR-175](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-175--a-cash-on-delivery-order-whose-customer-has-not-answered-three-hours-after-it-was-placed-asks-them-once-more-with-the-same-buttons-and-link-in-the-shops-calling-hours-a-sweep-in-the-worker-finds-them-and-an-order-placed-more-than-three-days-before-is-left-to-the-desk)), and the desk may wait for its answer before calling ([ADR-203](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-203--a-shops-confirmation-desk-may-wait-for-its-customers-to-answer-on-whatsapp-where-the-shop-asks-an-ordinary-cash-on-delivery-order-is-dealt-for-its-first-call-an-hour-after-its-reminder-to-confirm-or-three-days-after-it-was-placed-when-none-will-go-one-of-high-value-is-dealt-at-once-and-an-order-is-overdue-counting-from-when-it-fell-due)). Each message is paid for from the shop's credit in rupees as it is sent, WhatsApp's by its template's category and an SMS's by its parts; one the credit cannot pay for waits, a code is not sent, and what WhatsApp could not deliver is given back ([ADR-155](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-155--a-shops-messages-are-paid-from-credit-in-rupees-it-buys-from-hatti-with-an-invoice-of-its-own-each-is-charged-as-it-is-sent-at-what-it-costs-hatti-and-hattis-fee-in-a-ledger-kept-beside-the-balance-a-message-the-credit-cannot-pay-for-waits-and-a-code-is-not-sent-and-what-whatsapp-could-not-deliver-is-given-back)). A parcel shipped brings its customer the order's page, where its way shows, and one with cash to collect tells them each time it goes out for delivery what to keep ready for the rider; a message carries the link its order's messages carried while it works ([ADR-160](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-160--each-parcels-way-is-kept-step-by-step-as-shopifys-fulfillmentevent-its-couriers-changes-recorded-once-from-the-workers-tracking-and-staffs-for-couriers-hatti-does-not-follow-the-orders-page-shows-them-the-latest-first-in-english-and-urdu-the-shipped-message-links-that-page-and-a-parcel-out-for-delivery-with-cash-to-collect-tells-its-customer-what-to-keep-ready)). The shop hears of its own low stock on WhatsApp too, at the number it gives for Hatti's alerts ([ADR-157](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-157--the-shop-hears-on-whatsapp-when-a-variant-runs-low-on-stock-and-again-when-it-runs-out-at-the-number-it-gives-for-hattis-alerts-once-for-each-spell-of-low-stock-which-inventory-keeps-until-the-variant-is-stocked-above-the-threshold-again-the-worker-hears-each-levels-change-and-queues-the-alert-as-a-message-the-shops-credit-pays-for)), and of its bills with Hatti, which Hatti pays for and the shop cannot turn off ([ADR-169](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-169--hatti-tells-a-shop-on-whatsapp-at-the-number-it-gives-for-hattis-alerts-when-its-plans-next-period-is-invoiced-when-its-plan-ends-unpaid-and-when-its-message-credit-falls-below-rs-100-each-once-queued-with-its-messages-from-billings-events-at-hattis-cost-whatever-its-credit-and-never-turned-off)). Its staff hear of an order someone else gives them, and of a comment naming them, at the numbers they sign in with ([ADR-191](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-191--a-member-of-staff-hears-on-whatsapp-at-the-number-their-account-signs-in-with-of-an-order-someone-else-gives-them-and-of-a-comment-that-names-them-as--and-their-name-the-orders-events-say-which-the-worker-finds-whom-through-the-identity-login-and-each-is-one-of-the-shops-alerts-paid-from-its-credit)). Customers hear of store credit the shop gives them, and a week before a credit of theirs expires ([ADR-192](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-192--a-customer-hears-of-store-credit-the-shop-gives-them-with-what-they-have-in-all-and-a-week-before-a-credit-of-theirs-expires-with-when-each-credit-is-an-event-the-workers-sweep-marks-each-credit-it-reminds-of-once-and-both-go-as-their-orders-news-does)). A customer paying ahead hears the shop has their payment while the order waits to ship, in full or its advance with what is left for the rider; cash paid at the door is no news ([ADR-171](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-171--a-customer-hears-on-whatsapp-or-by-sms-where-the-shop-saves-that-the-shop-has-their-payment-while-the-order-waits-to-ship-once-it-is-paid-in-full-by-transfer-online-or-as-staff-record-it-and-paying-on-delivery-once-its-advance-is-in-with-what-is-left-for-the-rider-cash-paid-at-the-door-is-no-news-to-whoever-paid-it)). Where the customer gave an email at checkout, each piece of news goes there too, in the same words with the same link, sent by the worker through Amazon SES from Hatti's address under the shop's name, at no cost to the shop, laid out as its own, under its logo or its name in its colour, with the order's items ([ADR-198](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-198--an-email-of-an-orders-news-is-laid-out-as-its-shops-own-under-its-logo-served-at-an-address-of-the-apis-that-lasts-as-an-email-does-or-its-name-in-its-themes-accent-colour-with-the-orders-first-ten-lines-and-its-total)); the Admin API lists them with the messages, delivered or bounced as SES says ([ADR-197](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-197--an-email-hatti-sends-for-a-shop-is-delivered-or-failed-for-good-as-sess-notifications-on-hattis-sns-topic-say-identity-hears-the-topic-and-passes-each-notification-on-and-messaging-moves-the-emails-message-by-the-id-ses-gave-it-as-whatsapps-statuses-move-its-messages), [ADR-181](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-181--a-shops-customers-hear-of-their-orders-by-email-too-where-they-gave-one-at-checkout-each-message-about-an-order-queues-a-copy-for-the-address-with-the-same-words-and-link-which-the-worker-sends-through-amazon-ses-from-hattis-address-under-the-shops-name-emails-cost-the-shop-nothing)). Not yet: the confirmation sequence's IVR, a message when a delivery was tried, shops' own WhatsApp accounts, emails in the shop's own colours and logo, push and the inbox |
| Spike 1: Liquid rendering | ✅ Done: go | [Results](./spikes/01-liquid-rendering.md). `apps/storefront` renders Hatti Base, a Dawn-class reference theme in English and Urdu, from JSON templates, sections, blocks and section groups, with Shopify's common tags and filters. Pages take 2 to 6 ms at p50 and under 10 ms at p95; one process renders about 260 a second. Limits on nodes, time, output, memory and snippet depth leave a failing section out; lists are fetched a chunk at a time ([ADR-035](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-035--the-storefront-renders-liquid-with-limits-of-its-own-fetching-lists-a-chunk-at-a-time)) |
| Spike 5: RLS and PgBouncer performance | ✅ Done: go | [Results](./spikes/05-rls-and-pooling.md). RLS keeps every listing plan and costs about 0.1 ms per transaction. PgBouncer adds about 0.03 ms per round trip, and serves 1,024 clients where direct connections fail at 128. Fixed: timeout startup parameters that PgBouncer refused, and the relay's `LISTEN` behind a pooler ([ADR-021](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-021--pgbouncer-transaction-pooling-with-no-session-state)). Since: hot queries prepared by name, the products statement first, a fifth off the products page ([ADR-108](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-108--hot-queries-run-as-statements-prepared-by-name-planned-once-per-connection-every-pooler-in-front-of-the-application-sets-max_prepared_statements)), then orders and carts, each checked by the benchmark against shops of every size, which now sell: an order in under a third of the time, a quarter off the orders page ([ADR-111](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-111--orders-and-carts-are-read-through-prepared-statements-too-each-checked-by-the-benchmark-against-shops-of-every-size-a-prepared-page-writes-its-size-into-its-text)), then an order's timeline, checked on orders with timelines, a quarter off it ([ADR-122](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-122--an-orders-timeline-is-read-through-a-prepared-statement-too-checked-by-the-benchmark-on-orders-with-their-timelines-its-locations-loader-stays-planned-as-customers-statements-do)), and tenant transactions begin with their shop set in one round trip, a round trip less for every request ([ADR-107](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-107--a-tenant-transaction-begins-with-its-shop-and-limits-set-in-one-round-trip-begin-and-set_config-sent-as-one-simple-query-the-values-written-in-once-checked)) |

That is 1837 tests. They cover:

* RLS isolation at the SQL level, including a shop setting that must not leak to the next
  transaction, and 400 interleaved transactions for two shops on four shared connections; tenant
  transactions beginning with their shop and limits set in one round trip, which takes nothing
  unchecked, and a connection lost mid-transaction closed rather than handed on; a statement
  prepared by name from four callers at once through PgBouncer, which fails without its
  `max_prepared_statements`, and a Drizzle query prepared the same way, one statement whatever
  the length of its list of IDs; a page's size written into a statement only as a whole number;
* cross-tenant probes through the API, including every catalog, stock, location, order, customer
  and blocklist mutation;
* what orders made: lines keeping their variants' cost as sold, whatever it is later; the cost
  of the goods kept, units without a cost counted, couriers' charges, the cost of what was
  written off from parcels refused and not restocked, parcels lost and customers' returns, and
  claims paid, those still on their way back neither; gross profit, and profit, a loss when
  returns cost more than the sales made; and through the API, with the gross margin;
* sales by channel, by where orders' last visits came from and by campaign, most total sales
  first, items refused at the door taken off their source's sales, orders without a visit
  together and campaigns spelt two ways one, adding up to the totals; COD health's confirmation
  and delivery by the same; and through the API, both, the channel by its OrderSource value;
* where shoppers came from: visits kept by their browser's cookie, the first and the last from
  elsewhere, as a page's script keeps them and the storefront does for a cart permalink, the two
  writing the same cookie, checked in Chromium too; moving between the shop's pages or coming
  back to it straight changing nothing, visits older than 30 days forgotten, cookies it cannot
  read and referrers that are no web page ignored, staff's previews keeping none, and a discount
  link passing its campaign on; the core keeping them with the checkout and the order placed,
  where each came from (its tag, its ad's click ID, the platform or site linking to it, or
  `direct`) and its UTM parameters, within limits, and dropping visits too old, ahead of the
  clock or with no web address; a part split from an order keeping them; erasure clearing their
  pages and keeping where they came from; the customer's own export giving them; and through
  the API, `customerJourneySummary` as Shopify's, null for an order without;
* returns on their way, the longest first with their days and items, on pages with exact cursors,
  those checked in or cancelled and another shop's left out; counted on the home with what their
  items sold for; and through the API, listed and counted;
* an exchange sent at once for a return: a larger size, confirmed, its stock committed, paid by
  what was paid for the smaller less its share of the discount, the rest to collect at the door,
  the order refunded by exchange as much, its timeline and the exchange's saying so, and what the
  customer spent counted once; a cheaper one paid in full, the rest said to refund; refused for an
  order whose payment is not recorded, too few in stock, a variant twice, a refund by exchange
  through `orderRefund`, and cancelling the return; and through the API, the refund by exchange
  naming the exchange's number;
* a customer's return of a delivered parcel: units of its lines with their reasons, coming back
  to the order's location by a courier's tracking number, named #1001-R1, the order's stage kept
  and its timeline and events saying so; checked in with each unit restocked or written off, or
  all restocked when nothing is said, then closed; cancelled while on its way, its units free to
  come back again; counted among the sales report's returns; its note erased with its customer's
  details; refused for units not delivered, refused at the door or coming back already, a line
  not on the order or named twice, a location the shop does not have, and another shop's order
  or return; and through the API, the order's return status following, and readers refused;
* items of an order split off as an order of their own: the next number, the units given at the
  prices they were sold at, the discount shared by what they cost to the rupee, a delivery charge
  only if given, and the rest of the order as it is, confirmed, placed when it was and naming it;
  both orders' totals, sales tax and cash to collect worked out again, their stock where it was
  committed, and both scored as the customer's first order, again after a new address; the
  customer's link saying which order it is part of, and their stats counting two; a part split
  again naming the first order, and merged back into it; refused for nothing named, a line twice,
  not on the order or with fewer units, all of it, a delivery charge past the law's cap, another
  shop's order, one paid by transfer or in advance, or packed, writing nothing; and through the
  API, readers refused;
* an order's delivery charge waived and something taken off on the call: its lines and stock as
  they were, its total, sales tax and cash to collect worked out again, and its timeline saying
  what changed from what; the same charges changing nothing, and a transfer order's codes
  staying; refused for nothing to change, a price that is not one, a discount more than its items
  cost or below what was taken off for paying by transfer or online, a total below what was paid,
  and an
  order packed; and through the API, readers refused;
* stock as Shopify's inventory CSV: a row for each tracked variant at each location, named by
  handle, options and SKU, a product without options as Title / Default Title, with what is on

One test starts the built API exactly as production does and checks the exported spans, so a
dependency upgrade cannot silently break tracing. CI runs everything against Postgres 17 and
Valkey 8, through PgBouncer 1.22. The suite also runs locally against Postgres 16, directly and
through PgBouncer.

`tools/db-bench` (`pnpm bench:db`) is the spike 5 benchmark. It loads 1,000 shops, with their
orders, customers and carts, and runs pgbench, the application's own code and leak checks,
directly and through PgBouncer, and checks every statement the application prepares against
small, medium and large shops.
`pnpm bench:storefront` is spike 1's: it renders Hatti Base's pages from a sample shop of 201
products, and `pnpm dev:storefront` serves them at `http://localhost:4100/`, and each published
shop at `http://{handle}.localhost:4100/`.

A manual run on 2026-09-27 went through setup, migrate, seed, starting the API and the worker, and
querying with a Roman Urdu search. A product created through the API reached the worker **3 ms**
after the request finished. Both processes shut down cleanly on SIGTERM. On 2026-09-28 migration
0005 went onto the development database, the seed stocked two locations, and the running API
reported each variant's stock and whether it can be sold online. Later that day migrations 0006
and 0007 went on, and the seed's seven orders came back through the API at every stage, from
waiting for confirmation to delivered and paid, and a refused parcel checked back in. Then 0008
and 0009 gave the development database's orders their customers, and a new seed's nine orders
came back with eight customers, their delivery history, and an order from a blocked number
waiting for review with the reason on its timeline. Migration 0010 went on next, and the seed's
four segments and a preview came back through the API with the customers they should have.
With 0011, the seed's customers came back with their consent, its history, and a segment of
WhatsApp subscribers. With 0012, the seed's ten orders came back scored through the API, and the
large order of the customer who refused a parcel was waiting for review at risk 0.70, with its
three reasons on its timeline; the shop's policy changed and came back through the API. Migration
0013 first failed on the development database, whose customers made the backfill leave checks
pending (the empty test databases had passed); fixed, and a migration test now runs it over data.
The seed's customer found by her second SIM came back with both numbers and her three orders, and
a customer erased through the API left their completed order with its total, stage, city and
province, and an `erased` timeline entry. With 0014, a number revealed through the API came back
in the shop's audit log, naming the access token that asked. On 2026-09-29, 0015 moved the
development database's 25 confirmed orders to To pack. The seed's packed order came back under
To book, and bulk confirm, pack and tag through the API changed the orders they could and named
the cancelled and the shipped order they could not. Packing slips and invoices for the seed's
orders came back through the API on A4, 4×6 labels and an 80 mm roll, in English, Urdu and both,
and were checked in Chromium with their fonts. That check found Urdu headings aligned to the
wrong side of their columns and Urdu line spacing that pushed a 4×6 slip onto a second label,
both fixed. With 0016, the seed's completed order from Peshawar came back through the API still
completed and closed, partially refunded, with its customer's spending net of the refund, the
refund in the audit log, and the refund beside what was paid on its invoice. With 0017, an order
placed through the API without an idempotency key was refused, and placed once with one: the
retry got the same order back, marked as replayed. The seed shop's orders then exported through
the API, with Karachi times and whole numbers for the app that asked. With 0018, the seed's four
draft orders came back through the API: one waiting for its address, one confirmed through its
link, one placed after a bank transfer, and one whose printed link was opened in Chromium at
phone width, in light and dark. Confirming it there placed order #1014, confirmed and to pack.
That check found an Urdu sentence that moved a date's day to its far end, and an Urdu label
split across two lines; both fixed. With 0019, the seed's first order came with a link. Opened in
Chromium at phone width, it asked before cancelling, and confirming it there left order #1001
confirmed and to pack, with "Confirmed by the customer through their link" on its timeline after
the app's "Made a link for the customer". That check found a form
posted without its action when submitted without its button; the action moved into a hidden
field. With 0020, the seed's first draft went out before its address. Opened in Chromium at
phone width, its page asked for the address and number; confirming then placed order #1014,
confirmed and to pack, and the same link opened the order's address form, the number masked.
Later that day the seed published its shop's storefront, served from Valkey in Chromium at phone
width: its menu led to its two collections and all products, Footwear listed by price, and the
draft shawl answered 404. With the worker running, a product renamed through the API showed on
the storefront about 220 ms later, with its new description in paragraphs, and the worker first
published 19 older development shops from their waiting events. That check found a page's
section styles ordered by which section finished first, so a page could differ from one render
to the next; they now come in the order sections start. With 0021, the development database's
shops got handles. The seed's new shop answered at its subdomain, in curl and in Chromium at
phone width, with its five active products under all products; the sample shop stayed at
`localhost`, and a handle no shop has answered 404. An older shop, published before handles,
answered at its new one once a product edit through the API had the worker publish it again.

## Deliberate simplifications

Each item below is smaller than the target architecture on purpose, and each has a trigger for
revisiting it.

| # | Now | Target (architecture docs) | Revisit when |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Outbox is one table, with a partial index on unpublished rows | Daily range partitions, dropped after 7 days | Before launch, or above about 1M events a day |
| 2 | `control.shops` lives in the application database | A control-plane database, replicated read-only into cells | When the control-plane service is built (MVP) |
| 3 | Product and order searches, the storefront's too, use `LIKE` over a normalised `search_text` column, which no index serves: a storefront search reads all of a shop's active products (25 to 30 ms for 100,000), forgives typos in products' words only when nothing matches as typed, reading the shop's words again for it (160 ms for 100,000; [ADR-234](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-234--a-storefront-search-that-finds-no-product-with-every-word-as-typed-reads-each-word-none-of-the-shops-products-holds-as-the-shops-own-words-a-typo-or-two-from-it-a-typo-being-a-letter-added-taken-away-or-changed-or-two-swapped-and-shows-those-with-the-fewest-typos-first)), has no facets or synonyms, and finds products, pages and articles by kind rather than by relevance across them ([ADR-212](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-212--a-storefronts-search-finds-the-shops-published-pages-and-articles-beside-its-products-as-shopifys-does-by-the-words-each-keeps-folded-through-the-online-stores-own-search-in-the-core-products-then-pages-then-articles-the-kinds-shopifys-type-names-and-suggested-as-a-shopper-types)), with no suggested queries; an address may search 240 times a minute, suggestions included, however many shoppers share it ([ADR-046](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-046--storefront-search-asks-the-core-which-finds-products-in-postgres-as-the-admins-search-does-until-typesense)) | Typesense with per-shop scoped keys: typo tolerance, facets (SRC-03), synonyms and merchandising (SRC-04), pages and articles in results | Facets and typo tolerance (V1), or a shop above about 100,000 products, for which a trigram index would come first |
| 4 | Every API request looks up its token in Postgres | Short-lived cache with revocation fan-out | When token lookups show up in latency profiles |
| 5 | The worker wires handlers, and the module services the storefront publisher reads through, by hand, without NestJS DI | A NestJS application context shared with the API modules | When a second consumer needs module services (search indexing, webhooks) |
| 6 | One BullMQ queue for all domain events | Separate queues and worker pools (`critical`, `integrations`, `messaging`, `bulk`, `indexing`) | When a second real consumer arrives |
| 7 | Tests use CI service containers | Testcontainers | Only if we need versions or topologies that service containers can't provide |
| 8 | No rate limiting | Cost-based GraphQL limits per app and shop (Shopify-style leaky bucket) | Before any third-party app gets a token |
| 9 | Products' images are made at their sizes and formats by the API, behind the edge, the first time each is asked for; an image by URL is shown from its source until it is ready; no video, 3D models, crops or focal points, and an image is not replaced in place; HEIC photos are refused ([ADR-158](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-158--hatti-keeps-products-images-itself-the-worker-reads-each-from-the-shops-upload-or-fetches-it-from-its-url-never-reaching-a-private-network-checks-it-and-keeps-a-clean-copy-without-its-metadata-at-most-4096-pixels-a-side-the-api-serves-it-at-nine-widths-in-avif-webp-or-its-own-format-each-made-the-first-time-it-is-asked-for-and-kept-and-an-image-goes-from-storage-and-the-edge-with-its-media)) | imgproxy, or an edge worker, at the same addresses; images at the shop's own domain; video and 3D models; the editor's crops | With the edge, and the admin's media screens |
| 10 | Stock holds (`reserved`) have no expiry of their own | Checkout holds that lapse after 15–30 minutes, released by a sweeper | With checkout, which owns its holds and calls `releaseReservation` |
| 11 | The first location stays primary; no transfers or purchase orders; low stock is counted and listed at one threshold a shop, and alerts go to one number a shop on WhatsApp, once a spell ([ADR-157](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-157--the-shop-hears-on-whatsapp-when-a-variant-runs-low-on-stock-and-again-when-it-runs-out-at-the-number-it-gives-for-hattis-alerts-once-for-each-spell-of-low-stock-which-inventory-keeps-until-the-variant-is-stocked-above-the-threshold-again-the-worker-hears-each-levels-change-and-queues-the-alert-as-a-message-the-shops-credit-pays-for)); history lives in one table | A primary chosen in settings; transfers (INV-04), purchase orders (INV-05), push alerts and staff's own numbers, monthly partitions for movements | Push with the merchant app; the rest with multi-location merchants (V1), or above about 10M movements |
| 12 | A customer's order stats are worked out from their orders on every read, and a segment using order fields aggregates the shop's orders each time it runs; customers can't be sorted by those stats | A customers search index (Typesense) fed by order events, behind the same segment language ([ADR-023](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-023--customer-order-stats-are-worked-out-from-orders-when-read), [ADR-024](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-024--segments-are-queries-evaluated-on-demand-over-fields-modules-contribute)) | A shop above about 50k orders, or when segment counts show up in latency profiles |
| 13 | Each order ships from one location | Routing lines to locations (INV-10) | With multi-location merchants |
| 14 | Order numbers come from one counter row per shop, so a shop's orders are numbered one at a time | Unchanged for normal shops; flash sales go through Drop Mode (ADR-019) | If numbering shows up as a wait in traces |
| 15 | Confirmation is recorded by staff or apps; no WhatsApp, SMS or call outcomes yet | The confirmation sequence and Confirmation Desk (COD-01, 02, 04) | With messaging (spike 3) |
| 16 | Parcels are marked shipped, delivered or refused by hand, with a free-text courier and tracking number, but those booked with PostEx, which the worker ships and follows ([ADR-149](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-149--shops-book-orders-with-their-own-courier-accounts-their-credentials-sealed-for-each-account-each-booking-waits-in-postgres-until-the-worker-books-it-through-the-couriers-adapter-keeps-the-couriers-number-before-shipping-the-order-with-it-and-follows-the-parcel-by-asking-the-couriers-words-read-through-mappings-kept-as-data)) | Couriers booked and tracked through adapters, with normalised statuses (SHP-01, 02) | With each courier's adapter |
| 17 | Marking an order paid records the full amount at once; no COD remittance matching | Remittance statements reconciled against expected cash, per parcel (COD-10) | With courier integrations |
| 18 | A delivered parcel's items come back as a return staff record and check in, an exchange sent at once for an order paid already; there are no return windows or fees, and a return is not found by the tracking number on its label ([ADR-136](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-136--a-customers-return-of-delivered-items-is-recorded-by-staff-each-item-with-its-reason-and-checked-in-when-it-arrives-each-unit-back-in-stock-where-it-came-back-to-or-written-off-money-given-back-stays-a-refund-and-the-sales-report-counts-what-came-back), [ADR-137](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-137--a-return-may-send-another-size-at-once-as-an-order-of-its-own-paid-by-what-was-paid-for-what-comes-back-credited-from-its-order-as-a-refund-by-exchange-in-which-no-money-moves-the-door-collecting-the-rest)) | The shopper's return portal (ORD-08); reverse pickups through couriers' APIs; return rules | V1; with couriers' APIs |
| 19 | The blocklist and the risk rules hold orders for review; at checkout, the shop's rules ask an advance of orders scored high ([ADR-094](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-094--a-shops-advance-may-be-asked-only-of-customers-new-to-it-and-of-orders-its-risk-rules-score-high-such-an-order-is-asked-it-instead-of-waiting-for-review)) and take those scored at its limit by transfer alone, the shop seeing nothing of those turned to a transfer ([ADR-099](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-099--an-order-paid-on-delivery-that-the-shops-risk-rules-score-at-its-limit-or-above-is-not-taken-at-checkout-placed-scored-and-undone-its-page-asks-for-a-transfer-instead)); nothing asks for an OTP or blocks an order outright; the rules see only this shop's history, with fixed weights | The checkout risk decision's OTP and hard block (COD-03), a model trained on delivery outcomes (COD-06, V1), and a cross-store reliability tier (COD-07, Growth) | The OTP with messaging (spike 3); the model once there are outcomes to train on (V1); the network tier after legal sign-off |
| 20 | Customers ask the shop to erase their data or give them their file, and staff or an app do it, erasure at once or ten days on, the erasures waiting listed ([ADR-116](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-116--the-admin-api-lists-the-erasures-waiting-the-soonest-due-first-with-their-customers-who-asked-stays-in-the-audit-log)); nothing tells the customer when theirs is done; a customer's addresses are where their orders went | Customers asking for erasure and their file themselves, a message when one is done, saved addresses | The privacy work before launch; customer accounts (CUS-02); messaging (spike 3) |
| 21 | Masking follows fixed role presets, and apps see numbers whole; the audit log is one table, and records reveals, exports, merges, erasures and the risk policy only | Custom roles with a PII-visibility permission, protected customer data scopes for apps, monthly partitions kept 24 months, and every sensitive action logged ([security §2.1](../architecture/11-security-and-compliance.md#21-merchant-staff)) | Custom roles (V1); the app platform; above about 1M entries a month |
| 22 | Segment dates count days in Pakistan time for every shop; segments have no behaviour fields and nothing reacts to someone joining one | A shop time zone setting; storefront behaviour fields; automations that evaluate membership per event | A shop outside Pakistan; storefront analytics; automations (V1) |
| 23 | Consent is recorded by staff, apps, imports, checkout's boxes ([ADR-187](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-187--a-shops-checkout-offers-a-box-for-its-news-and-offers-on-each-channel-it-chooses-whatsapp-until-it-does-unticked-until-the-shopper-ticks-it-a-box-ticked-records-the-customers-consent-as-the-order-is-placed-in-the-words-beside-it-where-the-number-or-email-typed-is-the-customers-own)) and the online store's sign-up form ([ADR-189](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-189--shoppers-sign-up-for-a-shops-news-and-offers-on-whatsapp-through-its-online-stores-form-as-shopifys-customer-form-posts-it-the-storefront-sends-the-number-on-to-the-core-which-keeps-it-as-consent-from-the-storefront-in-the-words-the-form-showed-for-the-customers-main-number-and-the-form-comes-back-to-its-page-saying-how-it-went)); "STOP" and "band karo" replies stop the shop's messages but are not in the ledger yet; no push consent or email double opt-in yet | Replies in the ledger, push per device, confirmed email opt-in | Replies with campaigns; push with the apps |
| 24 | Imports and exports run inside one API request, capped at 5,000 and 10,000 customers; imports bring profiles and consent, not addresses or order history | Background jobs with files in R2 and progress; the full Shopify migration (F10) | With the infrastructure (R2) and the migration tool |
| 25 | An order's risk is scored when it is placed, when its address changes, and while it waits to be confirmed as its customer's history changes ([ADR-112](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-112--an-order-waiting-to-be-confirmed-is-scored-again-when-its-customers-history-changes-by-the-worker-a-score-that-makes-it-risky-holds-it-and-a-held-order-stays-held)); a confirmed order keeps the score it was confirmed on, and only this shop's history counts | Staff told when a confirmed order's customer refuses another parcel before it ships; a history across shops (COD-07) | If shops ship such orders and they come back; the network tier after legal sign-off |
| 26 | Bulk actions run inside the request, one order after another, up to 250 | Larger batches as background jobs on the `bulk` queue, with progress | When merchants select more than 250 orders, or a batch takes more than a few seconds |
| 27 | Packing slips and invoices are HTML pages the browser prints, from fixed templates, with fonts from Google Fonts; an invoice goes by its order's number, a sales tax invoice naming the shop's NTN and STRN where it gives them ([ADR-190](../architecture/13-decision-log.md#adr-190--a-shops-ntn-and-sales-tax-registration-number-are-kept-with-its-tax-settings-as-fbr-writes-them-and-its-invoices-name-them-with-a-sales-tax-registration-number-they-are-sales-tax-invoices-which-say-their-value-without-the-tax-too)) | PDFs from the documents service (Gotenberg) in bulk through the queue, to email or send on WhatsApp; fonts from our CDN; templates merchants can edit; FBR tax invoices in a gapless series (TAX-04, TAX-05) | With the infrastructure (Gotenberg, R2, the CDN); the series with V1, and FBR's digital invoicing with Growth |
